These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.\n- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.\n\nRequired diagnosis:\n1. Read the repository instructions, README, CLAUDE guide, package scripts, and current git status. Preserve all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked work. Stay inside the active standalone version directory.\n2. Trace the actual document and module versions that produced the log. The supplied source labels are v4.9 and v4.11, while local source identifies v5.0.11. Determine whether the browser is controlled by an older service worker or using a different deployment before editing. If that cannot be observed, keep runtime provenance unverified.\n3. Trace startup in order: module evaluation, early configuration handler binding, IndexedDB open and compatibility validation, application bootstrap, recovery UI, and gear activation. Identify the first failure or explicitly state that it was not reproduced.\n4. Review existing openIndexedDBCompatible(), DB_STORE_KEY_PATHS validation, bootstrap recovery, BlendDebug/logging, wireConfigPanelControls(), and navigation preload code. Current local implementations and tests may already satisfy some or all requirements. Do not create duplicate abstractions or claim a defect solely because it appears in the old log.\n5. Classify the blocked resource only after capturing its actual request URL and initiator. The opaque resource label alone is insufficient evidence.\n\nImplementation requirements:\n- Preserve all user data. Never clear or delete IndexedDB, request a lower schema version, blindly bump DB_VERSION, or create an undocumented database. If a VersionError occurs, use only the existing safe compatibility contract or implement a documented versionless fallback followed by validation of all required stores and key paths. Close and fail safely when the schema is incompatible.\n- Keep the gear available before bootstrap finishes. It must open the existing canonical configuration panel during pending, successful, and failed database startup. Keep pointer and keyboard operation, focus management, ARIA state, and the existing one-panel architecture.\n- Catch the promise that owns startup failure. Each startup attempt must settle deterministically as ready or failed, clear loading state, record the failed stage, and avoid unhandled rejections. Preserve the original error cause and expose a non-destructive retry path where retry is valid.\n- Add structured, bounded diagnostics through the existing logger. Include enough safe context to explain app/cache version, bootstrap stage, database name, requested and observed versions, fallback choice, schema validation, handler binding, gear activation, panel state, and final outcome. Avoid duplicate stack traces and high-volume event logging.\n- Keep logs free of tokens, signed URL parameters, private media URLs, local paths, filenames, user data, and file handles. Do not add a global handler that suppresses unrelated errors.\n- Investigate navigation preload across normal navigation, alias navigation, cache hits, and offline fallback. Use respondWith() for the response and waitUntil() for asynchronous work that must survive the response. Correct only the reproduced lifetime defect and retain existing cache and offline behavior. Do not disable preload or bypass the service worker to hide the message.\n- Do not treat ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT as an application defect until its request and initiator are known. Do not tell users to disable privacy extensions or browser protections. Correct the app only if the identified blocked resource is a required app dependency.\n- Reuse the existing modules and test conventions. Keep the app static and dependency-free at runtime.\n\nTesting and verification:\n- Add or update deterministic tests for each changed behavior. Cover positive startup with fresh and compatible newer databases, saved data preservation, pointer and keyboard gear use, recovery after transient failure, incompatible schema failure, and absence of unhandled rejections. Cover relevant service-worker preload and offline paths. Add no meaningless tests for unrelated dimensions.\n- Start with focused tests such as node --test tests/regression/indexeddb-open.test.mjs and the targeted indexeddb-newer-schema, indexeddb-startup-recovery, configuration-playback-controls, and PWA specs. Then run npm run check and npm run test:regression when the changed scope warrants them. Run browser tests only when available. If a Playwright process fails with spawn EPERM before discovery/assertions, report runner infrastructure failure with zero assertions, not a product pass or failure.\n- Use HTTP for manual browser and service-worker verification. Clearly separate static inspection, unit/fake IndexedDB results, local Playwright/browser results, and actual deployed or installed-app observations.\n- Build a requirement-to-test matrix containing expected behavior and a verification status for every criterion. Report exact commands, actual results, unrun checks, remaining uncertainty, and risks.\n- If inspection proves a requirement is already satisfied, verify it and report that evidence without adding a redundant implementation. Detailed master post-processed prompt

This complete prompt is the template-processed description for the overall refactor. The 20 issue prompts below divide it into focused, independently usable work items.

Role: Senior browser application architect and debugging engineer specializing in JavaScript, IndexedDB, service workers, accessible configuration workflows, and browser test automation.

Objective:
Diagnose and fix the reported Blend Player startup/configuration incident. The configuration interface does not open when the gear icon is pressed, the user sees no apparent activity, and the console reports a navigation preload cancellation, an unidentified ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT resource, and an uncaught IndexedDB VersionError where requested version 4 is below existing version 6. Add useful console diagnostics that show what the app attempted and where it stopped, then correct every cause that is confirmed in the active runtime.

Repository scope and baseline:
Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Required diagnosis:
1. Read the repository instructions, README, CLAUDE guide, package scripts, and current git status. Preserve all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked work. Stay inside the active standalone version directory.
2. Trace the actual document and module versions that produced the log. The supplied source labels are v4.9 and v4.11, while local source identifies v5.0.11. Determine whether the browser is controlled by an older service worker or using a different deployment before editing. If that cannot be observed, keep runtime provenance unverified.
3. Trace startup in order: module evaluation, early configuration handler binding, IndexedDB open and compatibility validation, application bootstrap, recovery UI, and gear activation. Identify the first failure or explicitly state that it was not reproduced.
4. Review existing openIndexedDBCompatible(), DB_STORE_KEY_PATHS validation, bootstrap recovery, BlendDebug/logging, wireConfigPanelControls(), and navigation preload code. Current local implementations and tests may already satisfy some or all requirements. Do not create duplicate abstractions or claim a defect solely because it appears in the old log.
5. Classify the blocked resource only after capturing its actual request URL and initiator. The opaque resource label alone is insufficient evidence.

Implementation requirements:
- Preserve all user data. Never clear or delete IndexedDB, request a lower schema version, blindly bump DB_VERSION, or create an undocumented database. If a VersionError occurs, use only the existing safe compatibility contract or implement a documented versionless fallback followed by validation of all required stores and key paths. Close and fail safely when the schema is incompatible.
- Keep the gear available before bootstrap finishes. It must open the existing canonical configuration panel during pending, successful, and failed database startup. Keep pointer and keyboard operation, focus management, ARIA state, and the existing one-panel architecture.
- Catch the promise that owns startup failure. Each startup attempt must settle deterministically as ready or failed, clear loading state, record the failed stage, and avoid unhandled rejections. Preserve the original error cause and expose a non-destructive retry path where retry is valid.
- Add structured, bounded diagnostics through the existing logger. Include enough safe context to explain app/cache version, bootstrap stage, database name, requested and observed versions, fallback choice, schema validation, handler binding, gear activation, panel state, and final outcome. Avoid duplicate stack traces and high-volume event logging.
- Keep logs free of tokens, signed URL parameters, private media URLs, local paths, filenames, user data, and file handles. Do not add a global handler that suppresses unrelated errors.
- Investigate navigation preload across normal navigation, alias navigation, cache hits, and offline fallback. Use respondWith() for the response and waitUntil() for asynchronous work that must survive the response. Correct only the reproduced lifetime defect and retain existing cache and offline behavior. Do not disable preload or bypass the service worker to hide the message.
- Do not treat ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT as an application defect until its request and initiator are known. Do not tell users to disable privacy extensions or browser protections. Correct the app only if the identified blocked resource is a required app dependency.
- Reuse the existing modules and test conventions. Keep the app static and dependency-free at runtime.

Testing and verification:
- Add or update deterministic tests for each changed behavior. Cover positive startup with fresh and compatible newer databases, saved data preservation, pointer and keyboard gear use, recovery after transient failure, incompatible schema failure, and absence of unhandled rejections. Cover relevant service-worker preload and offline paths. Add no meaningless tests for unrelated dimensions.
- Start with focused tests such as node --test tests/regression/indexeddb-open.test.mjs and the targeted indexeddb-newer-schema, indexeddb-startup-recovery, configuration-playback-controls, and PWA specs. Then run npm run check and npm run test:regression when the changed scope warrants them. Run browser tests only when available. If a Playwright process fails with spawn EPERM before discovery/assertions, report runner infrastructure failure with zero assertions, not a product pass or failure.
- Use HTTP for manual browser and service-worker verification. Clearly separate static inspection, unit/fake IndexedDB results, local Playwright/browser results, and actual deployed or installed-app observations.
- Build a requirement-to-test matrix containing expected behavior and a verification status for every criterion. Report exact commands, actual results, unrun checks, remaining uncertainty, and risks.
- If inspection proves a requirement is already satisfied, verify it and report that evidence without adding a redundant implementation. Do not use em-dashes.

Add useful debugging output to the console so the startup and configuration failure can be diagnosed, then refactor the application to fix verified causes and make the gear icon open the configuration interface.

The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. - Manual Testing: Use a local HTTP server, not file://, when observing modules or service workers.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.

Issue 03 / 20

Handle a lower requested IndexedDB version without aborting startup

Not Complete
Title
Handle a lower requested IndexedDB version without aborting startup
DTM
Ensure a request below an existing database version follows a safe compatibility path or produces a caught, actionable recovery state.
Kind
IndexedDB forward compatibility
Priority
P1
Severity
High because a rejected open currently appears as an uncaught promise and blocks normal startup.
Likelihood
VersionError is explicitly present in the supplied log; its source build and current local behavior require confirmation.
Impacted area
indexeddb-open.js, app.js openDB/bootstrap, pwa-config.js DB_VERSION, and IndexedDB regression coverage.

Failure mode

A lower version request rejects before startup can load saved state or present usable configuration.

Evidence or rationale

The log says requested version 4 is less than existing version 6. The active checkout currently declares version 5 and already has a compatibility helper.

Why this matters

Older app code must not attempt to downgrade a newer IndexedDB database or expose an unhandled rejection.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify and, only if needed, correct the app's forward-compatible open behavior for an existing newer database.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Audit openIndexedDBCompatible() preflight, VersionError recognition, versionless fallback, race handling, error wrapping, and all callers.
2. Keep the DB name and current schema source canonical. On a VersionError, a versionless open may be used only if it does not trigger an unintended upgrade and the returned connection is validated.
2. Handle both supported database enumeration and browsers where indexedDB.databases() is unavailable.
2. Ensure each open attempt settles once, timers are cleared, connections close on failed validation, and callers catch the final error.
2. Do not change DB_VERSION from 5 to 6 simply to silence the old log. Make an explicit migration only if the current application schema truly requires it.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Cover requested version below existing version, absent database enumeration, fallback success, fallback failure, and rejected opens.
- Database Testing: Assert the resulting version is not downgraded and existing records remain intact.
- Negative Testing: Verify incompatible fallback rejects with a classified error rather than continuing.
- Regression Testing: Protect the original requested-vs-existing VersionError scenario.
- Recovery Testing: Verify startup presents its defined recovery state after all safe open paths fail.

Acceptance criteria:
1. An existing compatible schema above the requested version opens without a downgrade or data loss.
2. An incompatible schema closes cleanly and returns a typed, handled error.
3. No VersionError escapes as an unhandled rejection.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Regression Testing: Protect the original requested-vs-existing VersionError scenario.

  • Recovery Testing: Verify startup presents its defined recovery state after all safe open paths fail.

Issue 04 / 20

Validate every required store and key path before using a newer database

Not Complete
Title
Validate every required store and key path before using a newer database
DTM
Prove the database compatibility check matches every store and key path required by the current application.
Kind
Schema compatibility
Priority
P1
Severity
High because using an unexpected schema can fail later or corrupt saved state.
Likelihood
Current source contains a key-path validator; coverage against the actual v4.11 schema is unverified.
Impacted area
DB_STORE_KEY_PATHS in app.js, assertIndexedDBObjectStoreKeyPaths() in indexeddb-open.js, schema migration code, and tests.

Failure mode

A versionless connection is accepted even though a required store is absent or has a different key path.

Evidence or rationale

The local source validates stores including library, playlist, slideshow, settings, experiences, thumbnails, dirHandles, aliases, and aliasMeta. The log reports schema 6 but does not describe its contract.

Why this matters

Database version numbers alone do not prove that an older app can safely operate on a newer schema.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Audit the complete store/key-path contract and fail closed when compatibility cannot be proven.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Compare all IndexedDB reads, writes, transactions, store names, and key paths in app.js and persistence modules with the validator.
2. Confirm the expected contract for every required store and identify optional stores only where source evidence supports optionality.
2. Test missing stores, incorrect key paths, transaction errors, and a compatible schema 6 containing saved records.
2. On mismatch, close the connection and surface idb_schema_incompatible with a safe recovery path. Do not mutate or delete the database.
2. Do not claim the live v4.11 schema 6 is compatible unless its schema is directly observed or owner-provided.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Cover all required stores, each key path, absent stores, mismatches, and validation exceptions.
- Database Testing: Open synthetic compatible and incompatible database schemas and verify records remain unchanged.
- Security Testing: Ensure diagnostics expose schema names and versions only, not stored media or credentials.
- Regression Testing: Keep forward-compatible schema-6 fixtures from losing saved library, playlist, and settings data.

Acceptance criteria:
1. The validator covers every store/key path the current app needs.
2. Compatible newer schemas are accepted without upgrade writes.
3. Incompatible schemas are closed and rejected without data mutation.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Cover all required stores, each key path, absent stores, mismatches, and validation exceptions.
  • Regression Testing: Keep forward-compatible schema-6 fixtures from losing saved library, playlist, and settings data.
  • Security Testing: Ensure diagnostics expose schema names and versions only, not stored media or credentials.
  • Database Testing: Open synthetic compatible and incompatible database schemas and verify records remain unchanged.

Issue 05 / 20

Keep database version and migration ownership in one canonical source

Not Complete
Title
Keep database version and migration ownership in one canonical source
DTM
Remove or prevent inconsistent database-version literals and prove migrations only run when an actual upgrade is required.
Kind
Migration consistency
Priority
P1
Severity
High because version drift can create startup failures and skipped or unsafe migrations.
Likelihood
The local code centralizes DB_VERSION in pwa-config.js; the old log may come from another source generation.
Impacted area
pwa-config.js, app.js migration handler, module versions, service-worker precache, and invariant tests.

Failure mode

A caller requests schema 4 while another source assumes schema 5 or 6, or an open path runs upgrade code against an existing newer database.

Evidence or rationale

Current pwa-config.js says DB_VERSION 5; supplied runtime reports requested version 4 and existing version 6.

Why this matters

A single version authority prevents asset/build drift and guards the migration boundary.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify all runtime database version values and upgrade logic are coordinated, and document any deliberate compatibility window.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Search the active checkout for every IndexedDB open call and every DB version literal, including compatibility entry points and tests.
2. Confirm pwa-config.js is authoritative and app.js consumes that value rather than maintaining an independent stale number.
2. Review onUpgrade behavior to confirm it creates or alters stores only for a genuine lower-to-current schema upgrade.
2. Keep the versionless compatibility path read/write-capable only after schema validation, but do not trigger migrations from it.
2. Update version invariant tests and documentation only when source evidence shows a real mismatch.

Issue-specific verification:
- Static Testing: Search for database version literals and assert active app modules use the canonical constant.
- Unit Testing: Verify upgrade handlers run for fresh/older schemas and not for compatible newer schemas.
- Regression Testing: Protect the no-downgrade and no-unintended-upgrade behavior.
- Additional baseline tests: Run PWA configuration invariants if any source version changes.

Acceptance criteria:
1. All active open paths derive the requested schema version from one canonical source.
2. Existing newer schemas are not upgraded, downgraded, or mutated by compatibility fallback.
3. A necessary migration has explicit tests for old, fresh, current, and newer schema states.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

  • Unit Testing: Verify upgrade handlers run for fresh/older schemas and not for compatible newer schemas.

Issue 06 / 20

Keep the gear handler available before asynchronous startup finishes

Not Complete
Title
Keep the gear handler available before asynchronous startup finishes
DTM
Make a trusted pointer or keyboard activation open the canonical configuration panel even while database bootstrap is pending or failed.
Kind
Configuration access
Priority
P1
Severity
High because users need configuration and recovery access when normal startup is blocked.
Likelihood
The local source already binds the gear before bootstrap; verify it against the actual DOM and runtime build.
Impacted area
index.html gear/panel markup, app.js wireConfigPanelControls(), bootstrap order, and configuration E2E tests.

Failure mode

The gear has no listener, is disabled/covered, or depends on a successful IndexedDB bootstrap before it can open configuration.

Evidence or rationale

The user reports no apparent activity. Current local app.js calls wireConfigPanelControls() before bootstrap and logs gear binding/activation.

Why this matters

Basic configuration and recovery controls should remain reachable during storage startup failure.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify early binding and correct any evidenced dependency that prevents the gear from opening the existing panel.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Inspect DOM IDs, initial hidden/disabled/inert state, event listener registration order, overlays, z-index, and any bootstrap gating.
2. Trace trusted click, Enter/Space activation, openConfig(), panel class/ARIA state, focus movement, and close behavior.
2. Keep one canonical #config-panel and existing control system. Do not create a duplicate panel or bypass the existing startup lock semantics.
2. If the existing early binding already works locally, test it under delayed, rejected, and blocked IndexedDB startup instead of adding another handler.
2. Report separately if only the old deployed v4 runtime lacks this behavior.

Issue-specific verification:
- UI Testing: Verify gear activation changes the canonical panel state and accessible attributes.
- Accessibility Testing: Cover keyboard activation, focus entry/return, aria-expanded, aria-hidden, and Escape.
- End-to-End Testing: Open configuration while IndexedDB is delayed and after it fails.
- Regression Testing: Protect the early-bound listener from being moved behind bootstrap.

Acceptance criteria:
1. The gear opens and closes the existing panel during pending, successful, and failed bootstrap.
2. The listener is bound once and no duplicate configuration panel or IDs are introduced.
3. Keyboard activation and focus state remain correct.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.

Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.

Issue 07 / 20

Make storage failure visible and give users a safe recovery path

Not Complete
Title
Make storage failure visible and give users a safe recovery path
DTM
Show a clear startup recovery state without hiding the configuration interface or risking saved data.
Kind
Startup recovery UX
Priority
P1
Severity
High because a silent startup failure leaves users unable to determine what happened.
Likelihood
The supplied report says there is no apparent activity; current recovery UI needs reproduction.
Impacted area
app.js recovery panel, startup status, retry controls, configuration transport lock, and recovery E2E tests.

Failure mode

The app remains blank or inert after IndexedDB failure, or offers a destructive action that risks local media metadata.

Evidence or rationale

The user sees no activity and an uncaught IndexedDB error. The checkout already has database-startup-recovery markup and retry handling.

Why this matters

A non-destructive, understandable state preserves user trust and makes diagnosis possible.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify and, only if needed, improve the visible recovery state for unavailable, blocked, and incompatible IndexedDB.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Audit the existing #database-startup-recovery message, retry control, locked transport behavior, and status announcements.
2. Show a concise message that says saved data was not changed and offers only a safe retry or documented next step when that is true.
2. Keep the configuration gear and panel usable; controls that require initialized state must be clearly disabled and explained.
2. Ensure retry reuses the supported bootstrap path, avoids duplicate listeners/timers, and updates UI on success or failure.
2. Do not offer clear-storage, database deletion, or downgrade as routine recovery.

Issue-specific verification:
- Acceptance Testing: Verify the recovery message, available actions, and result after success or repeated failure.
- Usability Testing: Confirm a user can tell startup is waiting, failed, or recovered.
- Accessibility Testing: Check role=status/live announcements, keyboard access, and disabled controls.
- Recovery Testing: Exercise retry after transient and persistent IndexedDB failures.
- Regression Testing: Confirm recovery does not erase or replace preexisting records.

Acceptance criteria:
1. A startup storage failure produces an accessible, actionable recovery message rather than silence.
2. The gear remains usable and state-dependent controls expose their locked state.
3. Retry can recover without duplicating bootstrap work or altering stored data.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Acceptance Testing: Verify the recovery message, available actions, and result after success or repeated failure.
  • Regression Testing: Confirm recovery does not erase or replace preexisting records.
  • Usability Testing: Confirm a user can tell startup is waiting, failed, or recovered.
  • Accessibility Testing: Check role=status/live announcements, keyboard access, and disabled controls.
  • Recovery Testing: Exercise retry after transient and persistent IndexedDB failures.

Issue 08 / 20

Catch every bootstrap rejection and settle startup state deterministically

Not Complete
Title
Catch every bootstrap rejection and settle startup state deterministically
DTM
Ensure each bootstrap attempt reaches a logged ready or failed state and no relevant promise rejection escapes.
Kind
Async error handling
Priority
P1
Severity
High because an unhandled rejection can abort initialization and obscure the original failure.
Likelihood
The supplied app.js line reports an uncaught promise rejection; current v5 bootstrap has a catch path that needs verification.
Impacted area
app.js bootstrap coordinator, openDB(), retry action, and startup recovery tests.

Failure mode

An async operation rejects outside the bootstrap boundary, leaving stale loading UI or an unhandledrejection.

Evidence or rationale

The console identifies an uncaught promise VersionError. Current local app.js logs bootstrap attempt started, failed, and settled.

Why this matters

Deterministic settlement makes the app state understandable and prevents secondary failures from masking the first error.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Trace all promises in startup and retry flows, then close any rejection path not already handled.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Map the complete async call graph for bootstrap, IndexedDB open, store hydration, event wiring, and recovery retry.
2. Verify errors retain their original name/code/cause and are classified without losing useful stack information.
2. Use try/catch/finally at the operation boundary that owns the failure. Do not add a blanket global rejection handler that silently consumes unrelated errors.
2. Ensure duplicate bootstrap requests join or are ignored consistently, and every attempt sets its final stage and clears loading state.
2. Preserve an actionable failure event while avoiding repeated logs for one error.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Test the bootstrap coordinator with resolved, rejected, duplicate, and retry requests.
- Negative Testing: Inject VersionError and ordinary IndexedDB failures and assert no unhandled rejection.
- Recovery Testing: Retry after first failure and confirm ready state settles once.
- Regression Testing: Protect the original unhandled VersionError path.

Acceptance criteria:
1. All startup promises are awaited or intentionally detached with rejection handling.
2. A failed attempt records failedStage and a safe error summary, and the recovery UI matches that state.
3. A later successful retry reaches ready without an unhandled rejection.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Test the bootstrap coordinator with resolved, rejected, duplicate, and retry requests.
  • Regression Testing: Protect the original unhandled VersionError path.
  • Recovery Testing: Retry after first failure and confirm ready state settles once.

Issue 09 / 20

Add structured, privacy-safe IndexedDB and startup diagnostics

Not Complete
Title
Add structured, privacy-safe IndexedDB and startup diagnostics
DTM
Record enough lifecycle context to explain requested/existing versions and the bootstrap stage without exposing user data.
Kind
Observability
Priority
P1
Severity
Medium to high because insufficient context slows repair, while careless logs can expose local or private data.
Likelihood
Current source already emits structured storage/bootstrap events; assess missing fields and noise.
Impacted area
logger.js, indexeddb-open.js diagnostics, app.js openDB/bootstrap, and diagnostics tests.

Failure mode

Logs show only a generic VersionError or print private values without explaining the selected fallback and final result.

Evidence or rationale

The reported line includes requested and existing versions but does not show the active build, startup stage, fallback decision, schema validation result, or final UI state.

Why this matters

A short, correlated lifecycle trace can show where startup stopped and whether the database was safely reused.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Audit existing logs and add only safe, structured fields needed to diagnose this incident.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Use existing logger levels and event naming. Include a stable attempt ID, app/cache version, bootstrap stage, database name, requested version, observed version when known, fallback path, validation result, and outcome.
2. Log both the initial VersionError and the versionless fallback result so the original error is not mistaken for final failure when recovery succeeds.
2. Keep diagnostic data bounded and avoid storing or exposing private URLs, tokens, media filenames, local paths, object contents, and file handles.
2. Avoid logging the same exception at every layer. Give one layer ownership of the error record and add context at other layers only when it changes the diagnosis.
2. Keep ordinary successful startup at debug/info level and actionable failures at warn/error level.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Assert event names and safe fields for success, fallback, validation failure, and rejection.
- Security Testing: Scan diagnostic payloads for credentials, signed query strings, paths, and stored record contents.
- Regression Testing: Ensure failure diagnostics remain available without changing startup semantics.
- Performance Testing: Confirm logging is bounded and does not enumerate or serialize the media library.

Acceptance criteria:
1. A developer can reconstruct startup stage, requested/existing version, fallback, and outcome from logs.
2. Sensitive user and provider data is absent from logs.
3. A successful fallback is not reported as a terminal app failure.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Assert event names and safe fields for success, fallback, validation failure, and rejection.

  • Performance Testing: Confirm logging is bounded and does not enumerate or serialize the media library.
  • Security Testing: Scan diagnostic payloads for credentials, signed query strings, paths, and stored record contents.

Issue 10 / 20

Trace gear activation and panel state transitions

Not Complete
Title
Trace gear activation and panel state transitions
DTM
Make it possible to distinguish a missed click, intercepted click, handler failure, and successful panel open.
Kind
Configuration diagnostics
Priority
P1
Severity
Medium because poor observability can conceal the reason a primary control appears inert.
Likelihood
Current app.js already logs handler binding, activation, intercepted coordinates, panel open, and settled state.
Impacted area
app.js configDiagnosticSnapshot(), wireConfigPanelControls(), openConfig(), closeConfig(), and Playwright diagnostics assertions.

Failure mode

The gear appears inert and logs cannot establish whether its handler ran, the panel changed state, or another element intercepted input.

Evidence or rationale

The user reports no apparent activity; local code already has gear-specific lifecycle events.

Why this matters

A bounded event trace can separate UI event delivery from asynchronous startup failure.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify existing gear diagnostics and add only missing, privacy-safe state needed for a precise diagnosis.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Audit current handler-bound, activation-received, activation-settled, panel-open, panel-close, and coordinate-interception events.
2. Capture only small state: trusted activation, input type, gear/panel presence, open state, aria-expanded/hidden, startup lock, and current bootstrap stage.
2. Do not log every document click, pointer movement, DOM subtree, event object, or sensitive user content.
2. Confirm capture-phase interception diagnostics do not alter event propagation or block legitimate clicks.
2. Test the log contract together with actual open/close behavior rather than asserting logs alone.

Issue-specific verification:
- UI Testing: Check gear event, open/close state, and accessible attributes.
- Automated Testing: Assert one activation sequence yields one bound/received/settled series.
- Accessibility Testing: Cover pointer, Enter, Space where supported, Escape, and focus return.
- Regression Testing: Ensure diagnostic listeners do not prevent application clicks.

Acceptance criteria:
1. Logs state whether the handler bound, activation arrived, and the panel opened.
2. An intercepted click is logged only with safe target metadata and no event propagation change.
3. The diagnostics do not duplicate handlers or produce excessive event noise.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Regression Testing: Ensure diagnostic listeners do not prevent application clicks.
  • Accessibility Testing: Cover pointer, Enter, Space where supported, Escape, and focus return.
  • UI Testing: Check gear event, open/close state, and accessible attributes.
  • Automated Testing: Assert one activation sequence yields one bound/received/settled series.

Issue 11 / 20

Expose a safe way to retrieve recent startup diagnostics

Not Complete
Title
Expose a safe way to retrieve recent startup diagnostics
DTM
Let a developer inspect the current startup status and recent relevant events using the existing diagnostics interface.
Kind
Developer diagnostics
Priority
P2
Severity
Medium because console output may be insufficient when startup fails early.
Likelihood
Current tests reference BlendDebug.getStatus() and getRecentEvents(); verify their availability and data boundaries.
Impacted area
app.js BlendDebug surface, logger.js persistence settings, startup recovery UI, and diagnostics tests.

Failure mode

Useful logs exist only transiently or the diagnostic interface is unavailable until after the failed bootstrap.

Evidence or rationale

Existing E2E tests read window.BlendDebug status and recent events; the request asks for debugging output.

Why this matters

A small, consistent read-only diagnostics surface helps distinguish build, DB, startup, and gear behavior.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify early-available diagnostics and provide a read-only status/event view only if current tools do not expose the necessary safe data.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Inspect the existing BlendDebug/global API and logger persistence behavior, including when each becomes available.
2. Expose only bounded status fields and a limited ring buffer of relevant app events. Keep it read-only and avoid serializing application state.
2. Make the API safe when storage, DOM, or bootstrap is unavailable; it must not throw or require successful DB initialization.
2. Document how to retrieve status and recent events from DevTools, including how to redact output before sharing.
2. Avoid adding a new debug UI or localStorage persistence layer if the current API and optional logger persistence already suffice.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Test bounded event retention, safe status in early/failed startup, and absence of sensitive payloads.
- Security Testing: Verify the diagnostic surface does not expose tokens, signed URLs, paths, or media contents.
- Usability Testing: Follow the documented console steps from cold start through failure.
- Regression Testing: Ensure production behavior does not depend on developer diagnostics.

Acceptance criteria:
1. A developer can read app/cache version, startup stage, DB outcome, and gear/panel state before and after failure.
2. Diagnostics are read-only, bounded, and safe when initialization is incomplete.
3. No duplicate or unbounded persistent logger is added.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Test bounded event retention, safe status in early/failed startup, and absence of sensitive payloads.
  • Regression Testing: Ensure production behavior does not depend on developer diagnostics.
  • Security Testing: Verify the diagnostic surface does not expose tokens, signed URLs, paths, or media contents.
  • Usability Testing: Follow the documented console steps from cold start through failure.

Issue 12 / 20

Resolve the navigation preload promise lifecycle warning

Not Complete
Title
Resolve the navigation preload promise lifecycle warning
DTM
Ensure navigation preload is deliberately consumed or safely accounted for before the service-worker fetch event settles.
Kind
Service worker lifecycle
Priority
P2
Severity
Medium because an abandoned preload can waste a request and hide a navigation routing defect.
Likelihood
The warning is directly reported; the exact route and handler path that triggers it are unknown.
Impacted area
service-worker.js navigation fetch strategy, alias routing, waitUntil/respondWith use, and service-worker tests.

Failure mode

The worker responds from cache or an alias route while the browser's navigation preload promise remains unsettled and is cancelled.

Evidence or rationale

The console says preloadResponse was cancelled before it settled. Local service-worker.js awaits event.preloadResponse in networkFirst but can skip preload for aliased navigation.

Why this matters

Correct event lifetime handling avoids abandoned network work while preserving navigation response and offline behavior.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Trace each navigation route and fix only the path that leaves a preload request unconsumed or untracked.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Inspect navigation route classification, alias resolution, skipPreload behavior, cache hits, networkFirst fallback, navigationPreload activation, and every respondWith/waitUntil call.
2. Determine whether the warning is harmless for a deliberately skipped preload or evidence that the worker drops a required promise. Do not treat the browser message alone as proof of user-visible failure.
2. When a preload response is intentionally used, await it as part of respondWith. When background work must outlive a response, register it with waitUntil before the event can settle.
2. For paths that deliberately bypass preload, use a supported explicit strategy that does not create an abandoned request, while avoiding duplicate network fetches.
2. Retain navigation aliases, cache behavior, and offline fallbacks.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Test navigation policy decisions for normal and aliased routes.
- Integration Testing: Exercise preload present, rejected, skipped, and delayed cases with a worker harness.
- End-to-End Testing: Verify root, nested, aliased, online, and offline navigation behavior.
- Regression Testing: Protect the offline fallback and cache routing behavior while removing the reproduced warning.

Acceptance criteria:
1. Every preload promise used by the worker is awaited or tracked by the relevant event lifetime.
2. Intentional preload bypass is explicit and does not create unnecessary duplicate fetches.
3. Navigation, aliases, cache hits, and offline fallback remain correct.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Test navigation policy decisions for normal and aliased routes.
  • Integration Testing: Exercise preload present, rejected, skipped, and delayed cases with a worker harness.
  • End-to-End Testing: Verify root, nested, aliased, online, and offline navigation behavior.
  • Regression Testing: Protect the offline fallback and cache routing behavior while removing the reproduced warning.
