Issue 13 / 20

Preserve navigation cache and offline behavior while correcting preload handling

Not Complete
Title
Preserve navigation cache and offline behavior while correcting preload handling
DTM
Prove that a service-worker lifecycle fix does not regress shell caching, alias navigation, or offline recovery.
Kind
PWA regression protection
Priority
P2
Severity
High if a preload fix breaks offline entry or serves stale app assets.
Likelihood
The app has explicit navigation preload and cache policy; regression risk exists if those paths are changed.
Impacted area
service-worker.js, pwa-config.js route policies, cache helpers, alias router, and PWA E2E coverage.

Failure mode

The warning is removed by bypassing the worker or changing strategy in a way that breaks offline or nested navigation.

Evidence or rationale

Current service-worker.js uses route policies, alias resolution, navigation preload, cache fallback, and offline fallback.

Why this matters

The worker is part of the app's local-first startup path, so network and offline paths must be tested together.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Add focused coverage around the actual preload fix and preserve all existing PWA behavior.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Review existing service-worker and PWA tests before adding coverage. Reuse the fixture server and route policies.
2. Cover a cached navigation with a slow preload, a network-first navigation with a completed preload, rejected preload, alias route, nested deployment, and offline fallback.
2. Check that an event response and any cache write/broadcast work are correctly awaited without extending event lifetime unnecessarily.
2. Do not disable navigation preload globally or change cache versions without a justified source change.
2. Report browser limitations if the harness cannot reproduce the browser-specific warning.

Issue-specific verification:
- Integration Testing: Validate worker response, preload use, and cache-write lifetime.
- End-to-End Testing: Run focused PWA route scenarios online/offline and at root/nested paths.
- Compatibility Testing: Verify supported worker clients and browsers retain navigation behavior.
- Recovery Testing: Open app shell offline after a prior successful visit.
- Regression Testing: Retain alias and media bypass behavior.

Acceptance criteria:
1. The preload fix passes relevant worker tests and preserves cache and offline responses.
2. No app media, range request, or API cache policy is broadened by the change.
3. Browser-specific warning status is reported only when actually observed.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Integration Testing: Validate worker response, preload use, and cache-write lifetime.
  • End-to-End Testing: Run focused PWA route scenarios online/offline and at root/nested paths.
  • Regression Testing: Retain alias and media bypass behavior.
  • Compatibility Testing: Verify supported worker clients and browsers retain navigation behavior.
  • Recovery Testing: Open app shell offline after a prior successful visit.

Issue 14 / 20

Identify the blocked resource before classifying ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT

Not Complete
Title
Identify the blocked resource before classifying ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT
DTM
Collect the failed request URL, initiator, and timing context needed to tell an app defect from a browser-side block.
Kind
Network error triage
Priority
P2
Severity
Unknown until the resource and its role are identified.
Likelihood
The console entry is observed, but its URL and initiator are missing.
Impacted area
Browser DevTools evidence, relevant asset references, logger/network diagnostics if applicable, and troubleshooting documentation.

Failure mode

A browser extension or privacy feature is mislabeled as an application defect, or a required app asset is ignored as an extension issue.

Evidence or rationale

The provided console line shows a short opaque resource identifier and ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT but omits the full request URL and initiator.

Why this matters

The same browser error can refer to an optional third-party request or a critical app dependency.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Establish the resource identity and whether it is required for configuration, startup, logging, or the service worker.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.

2. If the resource is an optional third-party request, document that the browser or extension blocked it and verify core app operation without it.
2. If it is a required first-party asset, trace the dependency and fix the app's URL, hosting, or fallback behavior.
2. Never instruct users to disable blockers as the product fix, and do not suppress all resource errors.

Issue-specific verification:
- Manual Testing: Record request URL and initiator from DevTools with sensitive values redacted.
- Network Testing: Verify required local app assets load and optional blocked resources do not prevent configuration.

Issue 15 / 20

Protect IndexedDB compatibility logic with focused unit regressions

Not Complete
Title
Protect IndexedDB compatibility logic with focused unit regressions
DTM
Make the helper tests distinguish safe fallback, invalid schema, unavailable APIs, blocked opens, and real errors.
Kind
Regression tests
Priority
P1
Severity
High because database compatibility is the trigger named in the reported failure.
Likelihood
A substantial indexeddb-open regression suite exists; identify uncovered cases before adding tests.
Impacted area
tests/regression/indexeddb-open.test.mjs and indexeddb-open.js.

Failure mode

The current helper regresses silently or tests pass without proving the old VersionError scenario.

Evidence or rationale

The local test suite already models VersionError fallback, schema validation, and higher schema states.

Why this matters

A focused unit contract catches schema-open regressions without needing user data or a live browser profile.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Audit current tests and add only missing deterministic cases for the exact compatibility contract.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Read the full helper and existing tests first, including fake request events and timers.
2. Ensure cases cover requested lower version, existing higher version with databases() available/unavailable, compatible and incompatible stores, racing schema change, blocked request, timeout, abort, and retry settlement.
2. Assert no downgrade, no unintended upgrade, connection closure on invalid schema, preserved error cause/code, and exactly one final outcome.
2. Avoid duplicating existing cases or mocking away the event-ordering behavior under test.
2. Run the focused Node test file and report its real exit status.

Issue-specific verification:
- Unit Testing: Run node --test tests/regression/indexeddb-open.test.mjs and add only uncovered cases.
- Negative Testing: Cover VersionError, incompatible schema, blocked, aborted, timed-out, and unavailable IndexedDB.
- Boundary Testing: Cover missing databases() support and concurrent version changes.
- Regression Testing: Verify the requested-v4/existing-v6 behavior is directly asserted.
- Automated Testing: Keep tests deterministic and independent of process isolation where project scripts require it.

Acceptance criteria:
1. Tests fail if a compatible higher schema is downgraded or an incompatible schema is accepted.
2. Every asynchronous fake request settles and leaves no pending timer or connection.
3. No redundant duplicate test is introduced where equivalent coverage already exists.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Unit Testing: Run node --test tests/regression/indexeddb-open.test.mjs and add only uncovered cases.
  • Regression Testing: Verify the requested-v4/existing-v6 behavior is directly asserted.
  • Automated Testing: Keep tests deterministic and independent of process isolation where project scripts require it.

Protect existing records in a higher-schema browser integration test

Not Complete
Title
Protect existing records in a higher-schema browser integration test
DTM
Prove a real IndexedDB fixture at schema 6 opens safely and preserves representative saved data through reload.
Kind
Browser database integration
Priority
P1
Severity
High because the failure relates to a user's existing database.
Likelihood
A focused indexeddb-newer-schema Playwright test already exists; verify it matches the current failure.
Impacted area
tests/e2e/indexeddb-newer-schema.e2e.spec.mjs, fixture server, IndexedDB helper, and bootstrap.

Failure mode

Synthetic helper tests pass but the complete browser flow fails or alters library, playlist, or settings records.

Evidence or rationale

The local E2E test seeds a schema-six database and checks saved library, playlist, settings, gear access, and reload behavior.

Why this matters

The issue crosses browser IndexedDB, async bootstrap, and visible UI boundaries.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Confirm the existing test reproduces the user-relevant path; close only evidence-based gaps.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Inspect the test setup, schema-six fixture, stored records, open-call instrumentation, and assertions before editing.
2. Verify boot reaches a ready state, saved records hydrate, gear opens via pointer and keyboard, and reload preserves records.
2. Assert there is no unhandled rejection, no forced upgrade, and no data deletion or parallel database creation.
2. If the test is already comprehensive, run it and report its result rather than adding duplicate coverage.
2. Keep this result classified as local synthetic browser evidence, not proof about the historical v4.11 runtime.

Issue-specific verification:
- End-to-End Testing: Run the focused indexeddb-newer-schema Playwright spec.
- Database Testing: Verify schema version and seeded records before and after reload.
- UI Testing: Open configuration after database hydration.
- Regression Testing: Protect the higher-schema startup path and saved state.

Acceptance criteria:
1. A compatible schema-six fixture hydrates expected records and remains unchanged after reload.
2. The page has no unhandled database rejection and the gear opens the panel.
3. The result is reported as local synthetic E2E evidence only.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

  • End-to-End Testing: Run the focused indexeddb-newer-schema Playwright spec.

Not Complete
Title
Exercise blocked, unavailable, and incompatible startup recovery
DTM

Kind
Failure-path coverage
Priority
P1
Severity
High because error handling must work when IndexedDB cannot open.

tests/e2e/indexeddb-startup-recovery.e2e.spec.mjs, app.js recovery coordinator, and database error classification.

Failure mode

Recovery works only for one error shape, while other open failures leave a blank or unusable app.

Evidence or rationale

Issue 18 / 20

Verify pointer and keyboard operation of the configuration panel

Not Complete
Title
Verify pointer and keyboard operation of the configuration panel
DTM
Confirm panel open, close, focus, and ARIA behavior for mouse and keyboard users across startup states.
Kind
Accessibility and UI behavior
Priority
P1
Severity
High because configuration is the requested primary workflow.
Likelihood
The local configuration E2E test already checks several states; coverage under failed startup needs confirmation.
Impacted area
index.html panel semantics, app.js openConfig/closeConfig, focus trap, and configuration E2E coverage.

Failure mode

A visual panel class changes but assistive technology or keyboard users cannot access or dismiss the panel.

Evidence or rationale

Current tests assert aria-expanded, aria-hidden, keyboard activation, Escape, focus, and startup panel behavior.

Why this matters

The gear must be observable and operable when ordinary startup is delayed or failed.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify the existing behavior and fix only reproduced accessibility or interaction gaps.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Inspect semantic button type, accessible name, panel role/label, aria-expanded, aria-hidden, inert state, backdrop, and focus management.
2. Test click, Enter, Space, Escape, close button, backdrop, focus entry, and focus return.
2. Repeat key interactions while IndexedDB is pending and after startup recovery is displayed.
2. Do not equate DOM assertions with screen-reader or WCAG conformance; report what was actually tested.
2. Avoid changes to modal semantics unless a real interaction or reader issue is reproduced.

Issue-specific verification:
- UI Testing: Check visible state and accessible attributes on open and close.
- Accessibility Testing: Run keyboard/focus checks and targeted semantic assertions.
- Usability Testing: Confirm the configuration path is discoverable and dismissible.
- End-to-End Testing: Exercise pointer and keyboard paths in the local browser fixture.
- Regression Testing: Protect existing gear, close, Escape, and focus return behavior.

Acceptance criteria:
1. Pointer and keyboard activation both open the same configuration panel.
2. ARIA state and focus are consistent with the actual visible state.
3. Startup failure does not make the gear unreachable.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • End-to-End Testing: Exercise pointer and keyboard paths in the local browser fixture.
  • Regression Testing: Protect existing gear, close, Escape, and focus return behavior.
  • Usability Testing: Confirm the configuration path is discoverable and dismissible.
  • Accessibility Testing: Run keyboard/focus checks and targeted semantic assertions.
  • UI Testing: Check visible state and accessible attributes on open and close.

Issue 19 / 20

Verify service-worker and app asset versions cannot silently diverge

Not Complete
Title
Verify service-worker and app asset versions cannot silently diverge
DTM
Trace cache and worker versioning so an old worker cannot keep serving stale app code after a source update.
Kind
PWA version consistency
Priority
P1
Severity
High if users continue receiving an obsolete build with the same failure.
Likelihood
The supplied v4 asset names differ from the local v5 version metadata; whether this reflects a stale worker is unknown.
Impacted area
pwa-config.js, service-worker.js, pwa-client.js, manifest, cache cleanup, compatibility entry, and PWA tests.

Failure mode

The HTML, imported modules, and controlling service worker belong to different release versions.

Evidence or rationale

The console logs reference v4.9/v4.11 while the checkout is v5.0.11 with a newer cache key.

Why this matters

Service-worker caches and old registrations can make a correct local code fix appear ineffective.

Implementation prompt

Role: Senior Blend Player engineer with browser startup, IndexedDB, accessibility, and test-automation expertise.

Objective:
Verify version coordination and update behavior without clearing user data or making deployment assumptions.

Repository and evidence context:
- Work only in the active standalone checkout at C:\GitHub\slideshow-playlist-player.html\src\blend.v5.0.11 - Copy. index.html is the entry point. This is a static browser app using native ES modules, IndexedDB, and a classic service worker. Preserve the no-build, no-runtime-dependency design.
- Read AGENTS.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, package.json, relevant source, tests, and git status before editing. Keep all unrelated staged, unstaged, and untracked user work intact. Do not edit sibling versions or the parent checkout.
- The supplied logs name logger assets v4.9 and app assets v4.11. The current local README identifies app version 5.0.11; pwa-config.js currently declares DB_VERSION 5 and asset/cache version 20261019-v5.0.11-session-and-recovery. Current local code already contains indexeddb-open.js with a versionless compatibility path and store/key-path validation, app.js with startup recovery plus early gear wiring and diagnostics, and service-worker.js with navigation preload handling. These are repository observations, not proof that the old runtime logs came from this source or that current behavior passes. Verify exact runtime/build provenance and current implementations before proposing changes.
- Treat every supplied console line as an observation to investigate, not as a confirmed root cause. In particular, do not attribute ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT to Blend until the failed request URL and initiator are identified.

Investigation and implementation:
1. Trace APP_VERSION, ASSET_VERSION, CACHE_VERSION, precache URLs, script query strings, worker GET_VERSION, and cleanup policy.
2. Confirm changed source files are cache-busted together and a newly activated worker can serve the matching shell and modules.
2. Preserve unrelated origins and non-Blend caches. Do not ask users to clear all browser storage as the first diagnostic step.
2. If stale code is demonstrated, implement the smallest safe versioning correction and test old-to-new activation and reload.
2. State clearly if production or installed-app behavior cannot be verified locally.

Issue-specific verification:
- Integration Testing: Verify app and worker version messages and precache asset resolution.
- End-to-End Testing: Test update, activation, and reload through the repository fixture.
- Compatibility Testing: Confirm the compatibility redirect and nested scope still load the matching assets.
- Regression Testing: Ensure cache cleanup deletes only Blend-owned versioned caches.
- Security Testing: Verify no other app cache or user database is removed during update.

Acceptance criteria:
1. Document, app modules, and controlling worker report a consistent local version.
2. A normal update serves the matching shell without clearing IndexedDB.
3. No hosted release or installed client is claimed as verified unless directly checked.

Safety and scope:
- Preserve existing IndexedDB data. Never clear/delete the database, request a downgrade, blindly bump the schema, silently ignore a VersionError, or create an undocumented parallel database. Only use a versionless open fallback when its compatibility contract is understood, then validate the actual schema before app use. Close and fail safely if it is incompatible.
- Keep diagnostics useful but privacy-safe. Never log credentials, access tokens, signed URL query strings, private media paths, file contents, or complete sensitive URLs. Do not suppress errors globally, disable browser extensions/content blockers, or weaken PWA caching to hide a warning.
- Reuse the existing logger, bootstrap diagnostics, compatibility helper, configuration panel, recovery UI, and tests when they already solve a requirement. Make only the smallest justified change. If current code already satisfies the issue, verify it and report that no duplicate code change is needed.
- Add or update deterministic tests for changed behavior. Prefer the focused regression and Playwright patterns already in this checkout. Run only applicable checks and report actual commands, outcomes, blocked checks, and what remains unverified. Do not claim browser, deployed, or production behavior from static inspection or synthetic local tests.
- Do not use em-dashes in generated files, code comments, documentation, test descriptions, or final reports.
Preserve unrelated working-tree changes. Never invent files, APIs, runtime observations, or test results. Never conceal failing or unrun checks. Do not use em-dashes.

Tests (where applicable)

  • Integration Testing: Verify app and worker version messages and precache asset resolution.
  • End-to-End Testing: Test update, activation, and reload through the repository fixture.
  • Regression Testing: Ensure cache cleanup deletes only Blend-owned versioned caches.
  • Security Testing: Verify no other app cache or user database is removed during update.
  • Compatibility Testing: Confirm the compatibility redirect and nested scope still load the matching assets.
